The files for FARM_Y to FARM_AH have no registered farm, so they're ignored.\n- **Reading the files:** they mix two text encodings, comma and semicolon separators, and European numbers like `18.000,0000`. I used the value column named in the calibration file exactly, trailing space included, because most files also have a decoy column whose name differs only by that space.\n- **Timestamps:** these are in each farm's local time, not UTC. Production runs from about 06:00 to 17:00 recorded time at every farm, whatever its timezone. So I converted them to UTC with each farm's timezone. Invalid stamps like `2024-13-40 88:99:00` are dropped.\n- **Duplicates:** each farm has 3 repeated timestamps. The first row carries a placeholder value of `888888.8` and the second has the real reading; otherwise the rows are identical. A plain `drop_duplicates()` would remove nothing, so I deduplicated on the timestamp and kept the later row.\n- **Calibration:** the true value is the recorded value minus the offset. Negatives are dropped only after that subtraction. Values are then converted to kWh, and power readings are multiplied by the farm's sampling interval of 5, 15 or 30 minutes.\n- **Safety rule:** no inverter temperature in farms A–X goes above 85 °C (the highest is 73.9), so the 45-minute shutdown rule never applies.\n- **Maintenance:** only the two completed windows are excluded. The Vistula (FARM_F) cleaning removes 12:00–14:00 Warsaw time. The Sakura (FARM_C) firmware update is on 16 March local time, but in UTC it starts at 23:00 on 15 March, so it removes the last hour of FARM_C's reporting day. The cancelled inspection is ignored.\n\nDecision for you: I treated each maintenance window as including its start time but not its end, the same convention the task uses for the reporting day. Only FARM_F has a reading exactly at the end of its window. If the end time should also be excluded, FARM_F drops from 3308 to 3154 kWh; nothing else changes.
